### The Tenth Ground of Proof.

That Duke Philip and his son Charles had the same figure and signature painted on all their banners and flags as that presented by the collar of the Order and its ceremonial habit: namely, a **flint** with **fire-steels** and **flaming rays of fire** emanating from them, together with the inscription: **It strikes before the flame shines.** <note>original: "ANTE FERIT QUAM FLAMMA MICET." This was the personal motto of Philip the Good, referring to the flint and steel used to spark a fire; the author interprets this as an alchemical metaphor for the preparation of the "secret fire."</note> This figure and device were not placed on the flags merely for the purpose of terrifying people, nor solely to honor the outward appearance of the high Order of Knighthood, but rather were intended to convey something else entirely.

### The Eleventh Ground of Proof.

A silver medal, which Duke Charles had struck, whereon one side is the bust-portrait of Duke Charles.

# On the Golden Fleece.

For if the fable or history of Jason’s golden pelt were to be cast out, and the story of **Gideon’s** dew-soaked fleece accepted in its place, then the entire name of the Order would necessarily have to be overturned and abolished, along with the Fleece itself, since these three are linked to one another, and none can exist without the others. ### Chapter 10.

From this, one may conclude that something particularly pleasing to God and Nature is hidden within it, which makes this Order not only pleasing and agreeable, but also causes its high-ranking members to venerate and respect it so exceptionally—a fact which certainly deserves further reflection. 

And it is by no means merely the story of **Jason** or **Gideon**, nor even the campaign against the **Saracens** <note>A historical term for Arab Muslims during the medieval period.</note>, but rather the good intent, the sincere spirit, and the goodwill toward God; for this great Duke <note>Philip the Good (1396–1467), the founder of the Order of the Golden Fleece.</note> sought to promote not his own honor, but the **Glory of God**. Therefore, if you would separate this single thing into two parts—as into Earth and Water—where the Water is volatile <term>volatile: a substance that easily evaporates or changes state; in alchemy, it represents the "spirit" that must be captured.</term> and the Earth is fixed <term>fixed: a substance that remains stable and solid even under intense heat.</term>, and then join them together again so that the Water becomes a Body <note>original: "Corpus"</note> and the Body becomes a Water, then you possess the entire Art. 

But if you cannot separate these earths from one another in this way, and reunite their Water with them again so that the Water becomes fixed and the Earth becomes volatile, then you have accomplished nothing at all in the high art. Therefore says Hermes: that which is below is equal to that...

### Chapter 11.

Knowledge. This fiery water rises from the earth into the heavens, into the alembic <term>alembic: from the original *alembicum*, a pear-shaped vessel used in distillation to cool and condense vapors.</term> and into the receivers <term>receivers: from the original *Vorlagen*, the glass vessels that collect the condensed liquid after it passes through the alembic.</term>: but it shall not remain there; instead, it must descend back into the earth. The separated fiery water, or watery fire, must be given back to the earth, for water is the life or the soul <note>original Latin: *Anima*</note> of the earth. 

Now, when the water has been separated from the earth, the earth is a dead body <note>original Latin: *Corpus*</note>, and entirely unfit for the bearing of fruit; if this earth is to bear fruit, then its spouse, its husband, its soul, its life, its spirit, its water must be restored to it—and indeed with great understanding, like a ferment <term>ferment: a substance that initiates a transformation, similar to how leaven causes bread to rise; in alchemy, it refers to the catalyst that "ripens" the matter.</term> of two opposing things, so that they may lovingly conjoin <note>original: *conjungiren*</note>, agree, mix, and coagulate <note>original: *coaguliren*</note> within themselves, purifying, washing, and making themselves white. 

For here it is a double work, a double marriage; there, the water makes the earth thin, flowing, volatile, and subtle; conversely, the earth makes the water thick, firm, fixed, and bodily <note>original: *corporalisch*</note>.

<summary>This page describes the alchemical process of circulation, where "fiery water" is distilled and then reunited with the "dead" earth to vivify it. This "marriage" of opposites symbolizes the purification and stabilization of the philosophical matter.</summary>

# Chapter XII.

## The Fifth Part, or Treatise

->Of our undertaking, regarding the two patrons of the New Covenant whom the great Duke, Philip of Burgundy, appointed over his new Order of Knighthood and founded in their honor, together with the conclusion of this little work.<-

At present, it is no longer a matter of questioning why and for what reasons the great Duke, Philip of Burgundy and Brabant, dedicated and established the high Order of Knighthood of the Golden Fleece <term>Golden Fleece: original *goldenen Vlies*; an elite chivalric order founded in 1430 to defend the Catholic faith</term> in honor of the Holy Virgin Mary and simultaneously the Holy Apostle Andrew, since this has already been partly addressed previously. # Concerning the Golden Fleece.

Y 5

I.) Mary, the mother <note>original: *Gebährerin*; literally "the one who gave birth to," a reference to the theological concept of the *Theotokos* or God-bearer</note> of the Son of **God**, **Jesus Christ** our only Redeemer and Savior, as our Lord, who, with his precious blood and holy life, has purchased us as his own possession and reconciled us again with **God** his heavenly Father; we who, for the sake of Adam’s sin, were enemies of **God**, lying in his wrath, curse, and death—as eternal damnation—and stood far removed from **God**.

This Mary was a daughter and princess from the royal house and lineage of David, the great King in Israel. Her father, however, was Joachim <note>While not mentioned in the canonical Gospels, Joachim is traditionally identified as Mary’s father in the 2nd-century *Protevangelium of James* and subsequent medieval tradition.</note>, a man who was otherwise well-to-do; and she was betrothed to a man named Joseph, a carpenter by profession. He was descended with her from the same lineage and tribe of the mighty kings in Israel and Judah, but both had at that time fallen into such reduced circumstances that they could not find much comfort in their royal nobility, but instead had to live quite poorly and in despised conditions.

## Chapter XII.
### The Fifth Part, or Treatise
#### Of our Purpose, concerning the two Patrons of the New Covenant, whom the Great Duke, Philip of Burgundy, appointed over his new Order of Knighthood, and established in their honor, together with the Conclusion of this little work.

At present, it is no longer a matter of questioning why and for what reasons the great Duke, Philip of Burgundy and Brabant <note>Philip the Good (r. 1419–1467), who founded the Order in 1430 to celebrate his marriage to Isabella of Portugal.</note>, dedicated and established the noble Order of Knighthood of the Golden Fleece in honor of the Holy Virgin Mary and likewise of the Holy Apostle Andrew; for this has already been partly discussed previously. Rather, our purpose, according to our promise, requires that we only set forth here how the Duke—with good right, well-considered counsel, and on an approved foundation <note>original: *approbirten Fundament*; referring to the legal and ecclesiastical validation of the Order's statutes</note>, as with a certainty—dedicated and assigned this his new Order of Knighthood to these two Patrons of the New Testament. Namely:

# Chapter 12.

This Mary was a pure and chaste maiden, sixteen years of age, who lived piously and devoutly and was betrothed to the Lord from her birth. This Mary, who was chosen by the Lord, was the first person of the New Covenant <note>original: "neuen Bundes"</note>, of the New Testament, and the first female Apostle <note>original: "Apostelin"</note> of the new apostolic kingdom. # Of the Golden Fleece.

Mary was by birth, and according to her name, a person of the female gender; according to her strict way of life, a person somewhat weakened in her natural powers <gloss>as written</gloss> and subject to the passions; according to her dignities, tender; but, according to divine properties, a strong nature. She was of sublunary <note>Meaning "below the moon," referring to the changeable, earthly realm.</note> properties, of a cold and moist <term>complexion</term> <note>In the medical theory of the time, "cold and moist" were the qualities associated with the humor of phlegm and the element of water.</note>, and of an earthly and elemental <term>humor</term>. However, she was also linked with an innate <term>central fire</term>, which lay hidden within the innermost parts of her body. Likewise, she possessed an <term>astral fire</term> <note>original: "Astrolischen Feuer"; refers to the celestial or cosmic energy believed to influence the human body.</note>, which awakened, moved, and brought that central fire to life, warming her and keeping her alive. 

She possessed, however, not only these two fires within her, but also a <term>lunar magnet</term>, which draws to itself the outpourings and effusions of the stars <note>original: "Astrorum"</note> and of the light of the higher workings as <term>essential powers</term>. This magnet maintains or feeds her in her state of health, just as the dear Savior himself says: "Man does not live by bread alone." <note>A reference to Matthew 4:4, suggesting that life is sustained by spiritual or celestial nourishment as much as by food.</note> In summary, <note>original: "In Summa"</note>

### Chapter 12.

Mary was, by reason of her birth, noble standing, and pious conduct, a tender woman subject to the passions.

Furthermore, this Mary was also a symbol of **Eve**, an image represented by **Miriam** <note>Miriam is the Hebrew form of the name Mary; in the Bible, she was the sister of Moses and Aaron.</note>. She is an image of the water that bubbles forth in **Mara** <note>A reference to the "bitter waters" of Mara in Exodus 15:23, which were miraculously made sweet.</note>; an image of the **First Matter** <note>original: "primæ Materiæ"; in alchemy, this is the formless, primordial base of all substance.</note>, of **Mercury**, and of the **Philosopher’s Stone** <note>original: "Lapidis Philosophorum"; the legendary substance sought by alchemists to transform base metals into gold and achieve spiritual perfection.</note>. Yes, she is an image of the **womb** <note>original: "Matrix"; used here in the sense of a creative source or mold.</note>, which catches the heavenly astral outpourings and leads them to the earth, thereby making the earth alive.

We say further, she is a symbol of **Eve**. For just as **Eve**, according to the mind and purpose of **God**, [bore] children of the Kingdom...

For although the male seed is of a fiery substance, the female sex possesses such a cold nature that the seed immediately stays and adheres to her, thickens, and becomes a body. It is the same with the **philosophical work**: If the solar seed <note>original: *solarische Samen*</note>, the **red gold of the sages**, is not sunken into the female lunar <note>original: *lunarischen*</note> white seed, or body, it cannot become a <term>Tincture</term> <note>A potent alchemical substance capable of transforming base metals into gold or healing the body.</note>. For the male seed, the **Philosophical Gold**, the **Golden Fleece**, must first dissolve <note>original: *solviren*</note> in the seed of the Moon <note>original: *Lunæ*</note> and then coagulate, thicken, and become stable—indeed, become an unchangeable body. 

6) In the same way, this can be said of **Mercury** <note>original: *Mercurio*</note>, who is set opposite to **Sulphur**: because he is cold and moist, whereas Sulphur is hot and dry. When they are joined together, like quicksilver and sulfur, they give birth to a cinnabar <note>original: *Zinnober*; a bright red mineral (mercury sulfide) often used as a pigment and central to alchemical experiments.</note>; for Mercury...

### On the Golden Fleece

<center>Aa 3</center>

**Doubt** may have moved the great **Hermes** <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure regarded as the founder of alchemy and hermeticism</note> to write his testamentary decree and the possibilities duty-bound to the laws of Nature upon such a gemstone, so that the **Children of Wisdom** <note>a common alchemical term for initiates or practitioners of the Great Work</note> might have before their eyes partly the description, but partly the matter itself, and might know what the **First Matter** <note>original: *Prima Materia*; the primitive, formless base of all matter required for the alchemical opus</note> truly is.

In this same way, **Philip III**, Grand Duke of Burgundy and Brabant <note>Philip the Good (1396–1467), who founded the Order of the Golden Fleece in 1430</note>, was able to establish and did establish this, his newly instituted high Order of Knighthood, the **Golden Fleece**, with every right and certainty of his true intent and purpose. While it was ostensibly founded in honor of the **Holy Virgin Mary**, the blessed **Apostle Andrew**, and also in honor of his wedding—under the pretext of undertaking a campaign against the **Saracens**—all these claims are merely outward, superficial reasons that have no real basis or foundation. Rather, the true ground, the primary cause, and the unshakeable foundation is that the great Duke was a true **Hermetic Adept** <note>original: *Adeptus Hermeticus*; someone who has mastered the secrets of alchemy</note> who possessed the <term>Philosopher's Stone</term> <note>original: *Lapidem Philosophorum*; the legendary substance capable of turning base metals into gold and granting eternal life</note>, as...

<summary>This page argues that the historical founding of the Order of the Golden Fleece by Philip the Good was a "blind" or pretext, masking the Duke's true status as an alchemical adept who possessed the Philosopher's Stone.</summary>

# Concerning the Golden Fleece.
Aa 4

...fetched the **golden Fleece** from **Colchis**. This **Great Duke** <note>Philip the Good, Duke of Burgundy (1396–1467).</note> did not adopt a new name to adorn his high order; instead, he contented himself with the **history of Jason**. He only renewed this order which **Jason** had established, knowing well what Jason had hidden within it <note>The author suggests that the myth of the Golden Fleece was an alchemical allegory for the creation of the Philosopher's Stone.</note>. And therefore, because he stood on the same degree and level as **Jason**, he did not wish to despise antiquity, but rather, in honor of **Jason** and his knighthood of that time, he wished to **renovate** the **Order of the Golden Fleece**, making it viable once again and bringing it back into memory, as has indeed happened.

Thirdly, [this was done] in honor of the **Virgin Mary** and the blessed **Apostle Andrew**. He took these two as the foundation because in this high **Work** <term>The "Work" (German: *Werck*) refers to the *Magnum Opus*, the alchemical process of transmuting base matter into gold or spiritual perfection.</term>, according to the state of glorification, there is nothing other than **brother and sister**; thus neither man nor woman; since such are equal to the transfigured **angels of God**. # Chapter 12.

...named twenty-five persons; and, as has already been mentioned, while he named these high-ranking knights publicly, he also kept twenty-five others in secret who were true **Adepts** <term>Adepts (*Adepti*): In alchemical tradition, these are individuals who have successfully attained the "Great Work," specifically the creation of the Philosopher's Stone and the transmutation of metals.</term>, in order to maintain the perfect number as a number rich in mystery. Just as seven times seven makes forty-nine, he—be he called **Jason** or **Philip**—was the fiftieth person. 

This number was already a number rich in mystery within the Law of **Moses**, where every seventh year was a year of release, and after seven years of release—that is, forty-nine years—the fiftieth appeared as the great **Jubilee Year** <note>original: "Hall-Jahr."
